## Document 039: 2026-11-11_［みずほ経済インサイト］堅調なAI需要で大幅増益が視野_1ea94428

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026-11-11_［みずほ経済インサイト］堅調なAI需要で大幅増益が視野_1ea94428.pdf
Report source: Mizuho
Date: 2026-11-11
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

堅調なAI 需要で大幅増益が視野 
投資増につながるかが焦点に

○ 製造業の業績見通しは大幅に改善している。輸出向けを中心とするAI関連財の需要拡大が背景。
2026年度は好業績が期待され、二桁増益が視野に入る 
○ AI関連財の増産を背景に、国内製造業の設備不足感は2025年後半以降強まっている。足元では関
連業種の機械受注が増加しており、AI需要拡大が国内設備投資に波及する兆しがみられる 
○ 設備投資は緩やかな拡大が息長く続く見通し。ただし、米国でデータセンター建設規制が実施さ
れれば、輸出・業績・設備投資の好循環が逆回転に転じるリスクも

１．企業景況感は AI 関連の製造業を中心に改善 
AIの学習・利用に必要なデータセンター投資や半導体需要が世界的に堅調であることを背景に、日
本の製造業の景況感は幅広く改善している。日銀短観（2026年9月調査）では、大企業・製造業の業況
判断DIが6月調査から改善した（図表1）。業種別では、半導体や電子部品などを含む電気機械や、半
導体製造装置を含む生産用機械の改善が際立つ。 そのほか、窯業・土石製品、 非鉄金属、半導体材料・
素材を扱う化学などでも改善がみられた。ロイター短観（10月調査）でも、「半導体関連市場の需要
が強い」との声が散見され、AI関連需要の堅調さがうかがえる。 
AI関連財の輸出が拡大し、足元で企業の業績見通しは大きく改善している。企業業績の改善を国内
の需要拡大につなげるうえで、次の焦点となるのが設備投資である。企業業績の改善に比べて、投資
図表 1 業況判断 DI 図表 2 日本の AI 関連財輸出額

### Page 2

2

などの内需への恩恵はまだ明確ではなく、今後設備投資の増加として発現する可能性が期待される。 
２．AI 関連財輸出は好調。2026 年度の製造業は二桁増益が視野に 
世界的なAIサービスの普及に伴い、データセンターや関連機器への投資が拡大している。大規模な
データセンターを保有・運営し、クラウドサービスやAI向けの計算基盤を提供するIT企業、とりわけ
ハイパースケーラーと呼ばれる大規模事業者を中心に、高水準の設備投資を継続する見通しが示され
ていることを踏まえると、AI関連投資の高い伸びは当面持続する見込みである。こうしたなか、モデ
ルの演算を担う処理装置のほか、大量のデータを蓄える記憶装置、サーバー同士をつなぐ通信ケーブ
ル、電力を安定供給する電源装置などといった財において投資需要が高まっている。日本企業が供給
する財についても、電子部品・機器への需要が増加しているほか、AI向け半導体の増産投資は日本の
半導体製造・検査装置や材料への需要を押し上げている。 
図表2は、輸出全体から、WTOの定義に従ってAI関連財を抽出したものだ。AI関連財輸出は2025年終
盤以降加速しており、8月時点では前年比＋29.1％と高い伸びになった。主因は半導体・電子部品で、
旺盛な需要のもとで半導体価格が大幅に上昇し、輸出金額を押し上げている。また、半導体製造・検
査機器や、データセンターに組み込まれる機械 ・ 部材、 半導体の製造に用いられる原材料 ・ 素材など、
幅広いAI関連財が輸出増に寄与している。 
輸出額の高い伸びを受け、製造業では業績見通しが改善している。 日銀短観（9月調査）の2026年度
経常利益計画（全規模・製造業）は、6月調査時点から大幅に上方修正され、前年度比＋11.6％の増益
が見込まれる（図表3）。特に電気機械では、上期の堅調な輸出実績を反映して、経常利益計画が大き
く上方修正された。このほか、業況判断DIの改善が目立った業種でも同様の傾向がみられた。堅調な
AI関連需要が下期も続けば、製造業では大幅増益が期待される。

３．AI 関連業種の投資需要は増勢加速の兆しも 
今後の実質GDPへの影響を見通すうえでの焦点は、 企業収益の拡大が設備投資を中心とした内需の押
し上げにどの程度繋がるかだ。この点、AI需要の追い風を受け好業績が見込まれることを踏まえれば、
設備投資の伸びはやや物足りなさが否めない。 日銀短観 （9月調査）によると、 製造業の2026年度設備
投資計画は直近5年間では最も低い伸びに留まっている（図表4）。建材や生産設備の価格上昇、金利
上昇に伴う資金調達コストの増加が投資負担となり、好業績のもとでも計画の積み増しを抑えている
可能性がある。 
もっとも、設備投資需要は計画対比で伸び余地がある。AI関連財を中心に増産が進むなか、能力増
強投資の必要性が高まっているためだ。AI関連財の生産は2025年秋以降、はっきりとした増加傾向が
続いている（図表5）。生産・営業用設備判断DI（全規模・製造業）も2025年後半から低下しており、
製造業の設備不足感は強まっている（図表6）。 
実際、AI需要の拡大が今後の設備投資を押し上げる兆しもみられる。 図表7は、設備投資の先行指標
とされる機械受注（国内需要向け）を需要者別に分解し、価格変動の影響を取り除いたものだ。これ
をみると2025年夏場以降、情報通信機械、電気機械、はん用・生産用機械といった業種からの受注が
急増していることがわかる。結果として、半導体製造装置をはじめとするAI・データセンター関連品
目では受注残高が積み上がっている（図表8）。 
ただし、機械受注が設備投資として顕在化するまでにはタイムラグを伴うとみられる。そもそも、
受注残高が積み上がっている背景には、旺盛な需要だけでなく、供給面の制約による影響があると考
えられる1。このため、設備投資の増加ペースは緩やかなものにとどまる公算が大きいとみているが、
その分、今後は息の長い投資拡大局面が続くだろう。

図表 5 鉱工業生産 図表6 生産・営業用設備判断 DI

（注） AI関連はWTOによるAI-enabling productsの定義に準ずる

### Page 4

4

４．今後は政府の投資支援策も民間投資を後押し。米国の規制強化リスクには要注意 
以上のように、先行きの設備投資は、設備不足感の強まるAI関連産業の投資需要に支えられ、緩や
かに増勢を強めると見込まれる。 
また今後は、政府の政策支援が、投資コストの負担軽減を通じて、設備投資を後押しするとみられ
る。7月末に始動した政府の「大胆な投資促進税制」では、一定の要件を満たす国内投資について、即
時償却や税額控除の適用を認める減税措置が設けられた。さらに、2027年度予算の編成では、特別予
算枠として『「強く豊かな日本」投資枠』を設け、民間投資を促す支援策が検討されている。具体的
な支援策が整えば、複数年度にわたる投資計画の実行を支えよう。 
なお、11月3日に投開票された米国中間選挙の結果を受け、米国におけるデータセンター投資が下振
れるリスクにも触れておきたい。同選挙では、データセンター建設計画の多いテキサス州やジョージ
ア州の州知事選などにおいて、建設の一時停止を掲げる民主党候補が勝利した。米国では、データセ
ンター建設規制に関して州政府や地方自治体が大きな権限を持つ。 
実際、市場ではAI関連企業の業績・投資下振れが意識され、グローバルにハイテク銘柄の株価が急
落した。仮に今後、米国でデータセンター建設が規制されれば、日本でも半導体・電子部品や製造装
置などの輸出が減少し、企業業績が下押しされる可能性がある。その際、AI関連財が減産に転じて設
備不足感が弱まれば、日本国内における能力増強投資の需要も後退しかねない。新知事の任期開始お
よび新議会の招集は2027年1月だが、当面の米国政治動静については注意深くみていく必要があろう。

1 なお、供給制約に起因して、資本財の輸入浸透度（国内供給に占める輸入品の割合）が一層上昇する可能性には留意が必要で
ある。今後、国内の供給余力が限られるなかで投資需要が一層輸入品に向かえば、国産資本財への需要が減退し、投資需要増に
よる GDP の押し上げ効果が限られるリスクがある。

## Document 058: 2026年10月12日國際經濟金融週報

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年10月12日國際經濟金融週報.pdf
Report source: Mega Bank (國際經濟金融週報)
Date: 2026-10-12
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引
用相關內容。

兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
中華民國一一五年十月十二日

國際經濟金融週報

### Page 2

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引
用相關內容。

1

國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
美國服務業與內需維持擴張，通膨黏著與中東風險牽動政策前景 
經濟 
⚫ 9 月美國 ISM 服務業 PMI 由 55.4 降至 54.9，連續第 27 個月維持擴張。
商業活動及新訂單雖較前月回落 ， 仍保持穩健成長 ； 就業重返擴張區間 ，
內需與勞動市場仍具韌性 。 惟價格指數續升創高 ， 出口與進口動能轉弱 ，
後續仍須關注通膨壓力及外需變化。 
⚫ 8 月美國商品及服務貿易逆差擴大至 1,056 億美元 ， 較上月增加13.7%。
出口 3,152 億美元、進口4,208 億美元，分別月增1.4%及 4.3%。出口成
長主要受非貨幣黃金、原油及半導體帶動，進口則以原油、黃金及資本
財增加為主 。 累計前8 月貿易逆差 5,570 億美元 ， 較去年同期縮減19.9%，
惟近期進口增幅高於出口，使逆差再度擴大。 
⚫ 9 月 FOMC 會議紀要顯示，AI 投資持續帶動經濟與就業動能，勞動市
場仍具韌性；惟去通膨進展有限，核心服務通膨及成本轉嫁壓力仍存。
全體官員支持升息 1 碼，傾向維持限制性政策，並關注 AI 融資、公債
市場波動及網路安全等金融風險。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 近期美伊緊張情勢再升溫，川普改口不排除選前對伊朗採取軍事行動，
伊朗則持續尋求重啟談判；同時葉門青年運動頻繁襲擊沙國目標，加劇
中東安全風險。若衝突擴大，恐影響荷姆茲與曼德海峽能源運輸，推升
油氣價格並增添全球經濟下行風險。 
⚫ 美國啟動 337 調查，針對 AI 伺服器垂直供電系統（VPD）相關專利侵
權爭議展開審查，鴻海、台達電、廣達等約 20 家業者列名。由於涉及
AI 伺服器關鍵供電技術，若最終認定侵權，相關產品恐面臨輸美限制，
對供應鏈營運帶來影響。 
短評 
⚫ 美國服務業與內需仍具韌性，AI 投資亦支撐經濟動能，惟貿易逆差擴
大、成本壓力升高及通膨黏著，使聯準會仍需維持偏緊縮立場。加上美
伊與中東局勢升溫，能源價格與金融市場波動風險上升，後續景氣不確
定性仍高。

歐 
元 
區 
法國殖利率短期內恐難降溫 
經濟 ⚫ 德國 8 月工業生產月增率為 2%。 ⚫ 法國 8 月工業生產月增率為-0.3%。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 西班牙政府宣布於 11 月 29 日提前進行大選。 
⚫ 法國 10 年期公債殖利率高出德國 130 個基點以上，主權風險升溫。 
⚫ 歐中談判最終達成 16 項共識，依據歐盟發布聲明，中國將自主減少混
合動力車對歐盟的出口量 5 成以上 ， 雙方同意降低部分產品MFN 稅率 ，
預期 2027 年 3 月雙方將再度舉行貿易會談。 
短評 
⚫ 考量法國財政不確定性較高，短期內德國與法國殖利率差距恐難縮小。 
⚫ 法國製造業在出口需求疲弱及財政支撐相對有限下 ， 景氣復甦力道恐仍
較為溫和，預料德、法兩國經濟及工業活動表現分化的情形短期內仍將
持續。 
日 
本 
薪資成長支撐內需，消費信心持穩

一、 美國—美國服務業與內需維持擴張，通膨黏著與中東風險牽動政策前景 
2026 年 9 月美國 ISM 服務業採購經理人指數(Services PMI) 為 54.9，較上月
55.4 下降 0.5 個百分點，已連續第 27 個月維持於 50 以上擴張區間，且高於近 12
個月平均值 54.1，服務業景氣仍持續穩健擴張。細項觀察，商業活動指數由 61.7
降至 56.5，新訂單指數由 60.9 降至 59.8，雖較前月回落，但仍維持高檔擴張；就
業指數則由 47.8 回升至 50.1，三個月來首度重返擴張區間。價格指數由 72.6 升
至 74.0，創 2022 年 7 月以來新高。供應商交貨指數由 51.3 升至 53.2，交貨時間
再度延長；庫存指數由56.7 升至 57.8，企業持續提前備貨以因應未來成本上升風
險。此外，未完成訂單指數升至 56.6，創 2022 年 7 月以來高點，惟新出口訂單
指數由 56.3 大幅降至 46.9，八個月來首度跌入收縮區間，進口指數亦由 56.3 降
至 52.9，外部需求及採購動能均降溫。整體而言，美國服務業景氣仍維持穩健擴
張，內需與就業展現一定韌性，但燃料價格上漲、關稅政策、供應鏈限制及地緣
政治等因素持續推升企業成本，後續仍須關注通膨壓力及外需變化對景氣的影響。 
2026 年 8 月美國商品及服務貿易逆差擴大至 1,056 億美元 ， 較上月修正後928
億美元增加 127 億美元，增幅 13.7%。當月出口 3,152 億美元，較上月增加 45 億
美元(+1.4%)；進口 4,208 億美元，較上月增加 172 億美元(+4.3%)。其中，商品貿
易逆差擴大至 1,366 億美元，服務貿易順差則小幅增至 310 億美元。出口成長主
要受工業用品及原材料出口增加 63 億美元帶動，其中非貨幣黃金、原油及燃油
出口增幅較為顯著，資本財出口亦受半導體、電腦及電腦周邊設備需求增加所支
撐；進口方面，工業用品及原材料進口增加 91 億美元，資本財進口增加 62 億美
元，其中原油、非貨幣黃金、半導體及工業機械進口增幅較大。累計2026 年 1 至
8 月，美國商品及服務貿易逆差為 5,570 億美元，較 2025 年同期減少 1,382 億美
元，縮減 19.9%；同期出口成長 11.8%，進口增加 4.4%，顯示美國對外貿易失衡
情況較去年同期有所改善，惟近期進口增幅明顯高於出口，使貿易逆差再度呈現
擴大趨勢。 
2026 年 9 月 FOMC 會議紀要重點如下：(1)經濟：若干官員認為美國經濟成
長動能有所增強 ，AI 投資規模與速度持續超乎預期 ， 帶動企業投資與生產力提升 ；

企業活動則受到企業獲利維持高檔及部分政策因素支持；(2)就業：多數官員表示
勞動市場近期略有改善，就業成長維持穩健，失業率仍處低檔，整體勞動市場大
致符合充分就業狀態，供需風險大致平衡；(3)通膨：官員普遍認為近期去通膨進
展有限，非住房核心服務通膨仍偏高，人力、能源及部分進口成本轉嫁情形仍值
得關注；部分官員擔憂 AI 投資與相關支出擴張，可能推升需求並增加通膨持續
性風險；(4)貨幣政策：鑑於經濟活動持續擴張、勞動市場具韌性且通膨仍高於目
標，全體官員支持升息 1 碼。許多官員認為維持較具限制性的政策立場有助降低
通膨風險，部分官員則認為未來可能仍需進一步緊縮政策，以確保通膨回到目標
水準；(5)金融風險：官員普遍認為金融條件整體仍支持經濟成長，但部分官員關
切 AI 基礎設施快速擴張所衍生的融資風險，以及美國公債市場波動、網路安全
與其他潛在金融穩定風險。 
近期美伊及中東局勢再度升溫，市場對區域衝突擴大的疑慮升高。美國總統
川普由先前宣稱期中選舉前不會攻擊伊朗 ， 改口表示 「仍有可能」 採取軍事行動 ，
且美軍已就相關軍事選項進行準備，使美伊對抗風險再度攀升；另一方面，伊朗
雖有意透過斡旋重啟談判，但雙方在核計畫與制裁議題上仍存重大分歧。此外，
葉門青年運動持續升高對沙烏地阿拉伯攻擊，利雅德哈立德國王國際機場一週內
多次遇襲，造成多人死傷，凸顯區域安全風險升高。整體而言，若美伊衝突或代
理人戰事進一步升級，恐衝擊荷姆茲海峽及曼德海峽等能源運輸要道，推升國際
油氣價格、加劇通膨壓力，並增添全球金融市場波動及經濟成長下行風險。 
美國國際貿易委員會(USITC)於 10 月 9 日正式啟動 337 條款調查，針對特定
垂直供電系統(Vertical Power Delivery，VPD)、 相關零組件及採用該技術的運算設
備是否涉及專利侵權展開審查。此案由美國電源技術廠商 Vicor Corporation 提出
申訴，主張相關產品侵犯其專利權，並要求發布有限排除令及停止侵權命令，以
限制涉案產品進入美國市場 。 調查對象涵蓋全球約20 家企業 ， 包括台灣的鴻海 、
台達電、廣達及其美國關係企業，另有中國的立訊精密、工業富聯、德國的英飛
凌等國際大廠列名 。 本案聚焦 AI 伺服器及高效能運算領域關鍵的供電架構技術 ，
該技術有助提升晶片供電效率、降低能源損耗，已廣泛應用於資料中心與 AI 運
算設備。隨著全球 AI 基礎建設投資持續擴大，相關專利布局與授權爭議亦日益
升溫。USITC 指出，此次立案僅代表調查程序正式展開，並不表示已認定侵權，
後續將由行政法官進行聽證及審理，預計於 45 天內訂定調查時程。台灣經濟部

則表示，337 調查屬企業間智慧財產權爭議，政府將持續關注案情發展，並於必

二、歐元區—法國殖利率短期內恐難降溫 
 德國工業生產在 7 月月減 1.2%後，8 月回彈 2%，主要反映部分汽車業者在 7
月提前進行歲修 ， 並且在8 月陸續復工後 ， 帶動生產及庫存回補需求所致 ； 此外 ，
受到 8 月萊茵河水位降低，沿岸化學工廠物流運輸受阻，致化工產業產出月減
1.2%為主要拖累因素。儘管工業生產如期反彈，惟工業訂單卻大幅下跌 10.6%，
主要是與軍工產業相關的訂單如飛機、船舶、軍用車輛、武器彈藥等訂單減少，
顯示政府前期大額採購效應逐漸消退；除軍工產業外，其餘產業訂單則大致維持
穩定。 
 法國 8 月工業生產月減 0.3%，為連續第二個月下滑，主要係夏季用電高峰過
後電力產出回落，帶動能源部門生產減少所致。另方面，製造業生產月增0.3%，
其中造紙、化工及電子電機設備等產業自前期低點回升，部分抵銷汽車等運輸設
備產出月減 2.2%的拖累。不過，法國 9 月 INSEE 新訂單指標由 8 月的-13.8 點降
至-15.8 點，顯示企業接單偏弱，主要反映出口需求疲軟。此外，相較於德國近期
透過國防及基礎建設支出擴大支撐工業生產，法國財政擴張力道相對有限，使製
造業景氣較依賴外部需求，德、法兩國製造業表現因而呈現逐漸分化的趨勢。 
 展望未來，近期歐元區各國殖利率上升，除反映通膨壓力增強、升息預增加
之外，亦部分反映西班牙宣布於 11 月 29 日提前進行大選、法國財政前景不明朗
等政治因素影響。儘管較高的殖利率有助於促使法國政府推動財政整頓，但考量
相關改革涉及福利及退休制度調整等政治敏感議題，不確定性較高，短期內德國
與法國殖利率差距恐難縮小。整體而言，法國製造業在出口需求疲弱及財政支撐
相對有限下，景氣復甦力道恐仍較為溫和，預料德、法兩國經濟及工業活動表現
分化的情形短期內仍將持續。 
三、日本—薪資成長支撐內需，消費信心持穩 
 8 月薪資年增 3.8%，其中經常性薪資年增 3.8%、加班費年增 5.2%、實質薪資
年增 1.5%， 主要受惠於春鬥加薪效應延續及通膨壓力較去年趨緩所帶動 ， 有助於
進一步提升民眾購買力，支撐消費與經濟復甦。

9 月消費者信心指數小幅下滑 0.1 點至 35.4，但表現仍優於市場預期。受食品

漲對消費的負面影響，顯示日本內需仍具一定韌性。 
四、中國—歐中達成貿易談判，外部風險暫時降溫 
中國 9 月外匯存底降至 3.40 兆美元，較前月減少 381 億美元，反映美元升
值、政府以外匯購買黃金儲備，加上歐美殖利率上升，造成中國持有的海外債券
資產出現評價損失，皆為外匯存底下滑的主要原因。惟亦不排除中國外匯流入情
況可能有所轉弱，例如出口成長放緩、進口需求增加，或資本外流規模高於預期
等因素。 
展望未來 ， 近期歐中談判達成16 項共識 ， 未來中國混合動力車出口至歐盟將
面臨調整壓力，但歐盟進一步升高貿易保護措施的風險已降溫。另一方面，基於
中國維持大額經常帳順差、出口商結匯需求及年底季節性資金流入支撐，預期人
民幣仍有升值空間，並帶動外匯存底回升。 
五、印度及東南亞—世銀上調經濟展望；印度央行近四年來首度升息 
(1)世界銀行 2026 年 10 月《東亞與太平洋地區經濟展望報告》指出，受人工智慧
（AI） 相關出口與投資強勁帶動，將2026 年東亞與太平洋地區 （EAP） 經濟成長
率預測上修 0.3 個百分點至 4.5%，2027 年與 2028 年將分別放緩至 4.4%與 4.3%，
其中，越南、馬來西亞、泰國 GDP 大幅上調至 7.4%、5.1%及 2.0%。世銀指出，
AI 相關商品占多數經濟體出口增長的一半以上，其中，馬來西亞、菲律賓、泰國
和越南的 AI 出口貢獻率更超過七成，但扣除 AI 相關產品後，區域貿易成長呈現
疲弱或負增長，警告該地區的經濟韌性高度依賴 AI 相關製造業和出口，使其容
易受到全球科技支出逆轉的影響，建議應將 AI 產品生產優勢轉化為廣泛應用，
以提升生產力，並創造更多優質就業機會。此外，美伊戰爭推升能源價格，各國
政府比其他地區更積極因應 ， 也更仰賴補貼 ， 此將增加財政成本並減少外匯存底 ，
世銀預估中東石油出口要到 2027 年中才會恢復至戰前水準，能源成本上升已對
製造業造成壓力 ， 運輸價格大漲也已打擊消費需求 ， 持續性的補貼可能難以為繼 。

(2)印度:印度央行 10/7 貨幣政策會議宣布升息 1 碼至 5.5%，為近四年來首度升
息，貨幣政策立場從「中性」調整為「有節制的緊縮」。央行總裁指出，目前已
有跡象顯示，通膨預期升高，且價格壓力正逐漸擴散至更廣泛的領域。經濟展望
方面，印度內需、投資及基礎建設支出持續支撐景氣，央行將 2026/27 財年經濟
成長率預測由 6.7%上調至 7.1%，另受中東局勢緊張、國際油價上漲、食品價格
攀升及氣候因素影響，將 2026/27 財年 CPI 通膨率預測上調至 5.2%，高於 4%的
中期目標。另外，世界銀行 10 月最新《南亞經濟展望》報告，上修印度 2026/27
財年經濟成長率 0.4 個百分點至 7.1%，反映內需消費、製造業、服務業及出口表
現優於預期，然而，中東衝突導致能源價格上漲、聖嬰現象衝擊農業生產、全球
金融市場波動及資本流動風險，仍可能影響經濟前景。整體而言，印度經濟成長
動能仍穩健，但外部風險及輸入性通膨壓力增加，未來貨幣政策將以維持物價穩
定為優先，並有進一步升息的可能。 
 (3)越南: 2026 年第三季經濟成長年增率達 9.95%，高於上季修正後的 8.81%，並
創 4 年新高，累計前三季經濟成長率達 9.01%。成長動能主要來自製造業與營建
業擴張、外人直接投資持續流入、以及外貿大幅成長。各產業均穩健成長，工業
及建築業成長 12.50%(占 GDP 總值 51.57%)；服務業成長 9.54%(占 43.29%)；農
業成長 4.21%(占 5.14%)。以支出面觀之，民間消費成長 8.96%，固定投資成長
21.39%，貨物和服務出、進口分別成長23.27%、28.75%。展望未來，越南受惠於
全球企業分散供應鏈、AI 產業需求及政府推動基礎建設與數位轉型 ， 經濟前景仍
偏正向。

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引
用相關內容。

9

六、台灣—出口動能強勁，物價壓力與貿易風險並存 
9 月消費者物價指數(CPI)年增率升至 2.73%，創近兩年半新高，已連續 5 個
月高於 2%警戒線，主要係受豪雨影響，蔬果供應減少帶動價格上漲，加上汽機
車減稅效應淡化、中秋節落點不同及國際油價維持高檔，共同推升物價。除蔬果
外，外食費、房租、電費及交通相關費用皆持續上漲，顯示物價壓力仍在，但主
計總處認為整體漲勢仍屬溫和，預估 10 月 CPI 將略為回落至約 2.5%。 
9 月出口額達 872.2 億美元，年增 60.9%，其中資通產品出口倍增、電子零組
件年增 45.3%。財政部指出，在AI 相關投資持續、部分電子產品供應偏緊、新品
上市與旺季拉貨等因素共同挹注下，出口表現明顯升溫；隨著 AI 應用擴大及年
底採購需求增加，全年出口額有望突破 9,000 億美元。 
美國國際貿易委員會 （USITC）於10 月 9 日依據 《1930 年關稅法》 第337 條
款，對特定垂直供電系統及相關產品啟動專利侵權調查，其中台達電、廣達及鴻
海旗下相關企業等遭列名。若 337 條款調查最終認定侵權，相關產品可能遭禁止
輸入美國，後續應關注調查結果及對台灣電源供應與伺服器供應鏈的影響。

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 美國 9 月通膨數據高於市場預期，CPI 月增 0.6%、年增 3.7%；核心 CPI 月增
0.3%、年增 2.6%。同月 PPI 月增 1.3%，創 2022 年 3 月以來最大增幅，年增
6.3%， 顯示通膨壓力已逐步由能源價格擴散至更廣泛領域 。 數據公布後 ， 市場一
度提高對聯準會 10 月升息的預期，但隨後多位官員釋出審慎訊號，使升息押注
降溫 。 聯準會理事鮑曼表示 ， 決策前仍有時間觀察先前升息效果 ； 舊金山聯準銀
行總裁戴莉則認為 10 月無立即升息之必要。而褐皮書顯示，美國就業市場維持
「低招聘、低裁員」格局，而高能源價格帶來的通膨壓力仍值得關注。
⚫ 中國 9 月通膨持續回升，CPI 年增 0.9%，創近三個月高點；PPI 年增 5.0%，創
逾四年最大漲幅，顯示通縮壓力進一步減輕。主要受能源與原物料價格上漲帶
動，且 9 月進出口表現優於預期，AI 產業鏈需求與貿易環境改善提供支撐；不

過，信貸增長仍仰賴政府發債與季底效應，內需與民間融資動能仍偏弱。
⚫ 日本銀行審議委員小枝淳子表示 ， 不宜對升息過於保守 ， 並認為政策利率仍有進
一步上調空間。總裁植田和男則強調維持2%通膨目標的重要性。整體而言，日
本央行持續釋出偏鷹派訊號，有助支撐日圓表現。
⚫ 歐洲央行管委溫施表示 ， 在能源價格波動下 ，為了控制通膨仍不排除進一步升息
可能，總裁拉加德則認為法國債務問題應透過財政政策解決， 歐洲央行官員普遍
認為能源價格的潛在波動依然是推升通膨並使其居高不下的主要風險來源。。
⚫ 澳洲央行會議紀錄指出，能源價格上漲與需求韌性可能使通膨更具黏性，保留
進一步升息空間。然而 9 月就業數據意外疲弱，市場大幅下調升息預期，目前
預估 11 月維持利率不變的機率接近 90%。

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
美元兌新台幣匯率走勢
Oct/19/2026
＜上週走勢＞
上週台幣匯價高點為 31.890，低點在 32.520，以 32.338 作收，較前一周收盤價
貶值 4 角 6.3 分。
上週台幣走貶 ， 周初 ， 外資延續前周大幅匯出的走勢 ， 雖逢高吸引出口商拋匯 ，
仍不敵外資匯出力道令台幣走貶 ； 週二台股重挫近千點 ， 外資大幅賣超台股1653 億
並匯出，台幣接連跌破 32.000、32.100 及 32.200，總成交量 71.3 億，創歷史新高；
周三台股收漲，外資雙向進出，台幣收斂部分跌幅；週四，受美國通膨高於預期影
響，早盤台幣跳空開低，台股跌逾2%，外資大幅賣超 3025 億，台幣跌破 31.400 關
卡 ， 總成交量91.2 億 ， 再創歷史新高 ； 週五外資持續匯出 ， 台幣貶至周內低點32.520，
終場在央行調節之下收在 32.338。
＜未來分析＞
預估本週台幣走勢偏弱。就國際市場而言，美國 CPI、PPI 雙雙高於預期，通

膨壓力仍在，儘管聯準會官員陸續發言表示不急於升息，淡化市場對於聯準會十月
升息預期，惟年底前聯準會仍有升息一碼空間，利率持穩於高位將給予國際美元支
撐；就台灣市場來看，市場對AI 產業資本支出、融資成本及信用風險產生疑慮，若
賣壓延續，將壓抑市場風險偏好，增加外資調整台股部位及匯出的壓力，令台幣面
臨持續貶值的壓力，惟出口商拋匯將抑制台幣貶值速度，綜合以上，預估本週台幣
走勢偏弱。
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：32.100-32.700

美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Oct/19/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣震盪走升。上週人民幣中間價連續走升，週三中間價升至6.7249 創
三年八個月新高，惟市場靜待美國公布通膨數據，交投謹慎，人民幣僅小幅走升；
週三晚間，美國公布 9 月 CPI 年增率 3.7%、核心 CPI 年增 2.6%，雙雙高於預期，
帶動美元指數升至年內高點 102.945、 人民幣貶至周內低點6.7005； 然多位聯準會官
員發言表示聯準會不急於升息，令市場對於聯準會十月升息預期略有降溫，美元指
數自高點回落，激勵人民幣由貶轉升；臨近周末，聯準會主席華許於IMF 會議發言
並未指引十月聯準會政策利率走向，再度淡化市場對於聯準會十月升息預期，國際
美元走勢偏弱，人民幣走升觸及年內高點 6.6825，終場境外人民幣收在 6.6850，週
線升值 91 點，境內人民幣則收在 6.6870，週線升值 60 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣震盪偏升。就國際市場來看，美國仍面臨高通膨、年底可能將
再次升息，利差因素支撐美元指數於高檔震盪，限縮人民幣升值空間；就中國國內
市場來看，中間價持續偏升將帶動人民幣走升，加以出口商實質拋匯需求將支撐人
民幣表現；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH 日線圖中，匯價維持在五日均線之下交
投 ， 均線呈現空頭排列 ， 加以USD/CNH 有效跌破 6.6900 整數關卡 ， 顯示USD/CNH
空方勢力較盛，綜合以上，預估本週人民幣震盪偏升。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6500-6.7000

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Oct/19/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 USD/JPY 先上後下。週初，受油價攀升推升美國通膨與利率預期帶動，美
元指數溫和走升創 6 月底以來新高，推升 USD/JPY 突破 158.50；週間，美國公布 9
月 CPI 高於預期，帶動美元指數升至 2025 年 5 月以來高點，也使 USD/JPY 最高來
到 158.94，然逢高獲利了結賣壓出籠使匯價回落；臨近週末，USD/JPY 一度下探至
157.53，隨後逢低買盤進場收復部分跌幅，終場收在 157.99，週線下跌 13 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、受美伊戰事推升汽柴油飆漲
與服務成本擴散影響，美國 9 月 CPI 及 PPI 月增漲幅均超出市場預期，加上初請失
業金人數連五週低於 20 萬人顯示勞動市場穩定，市場預期聯準會將於 12 月再次升
息，美日實質利差維持高檔，持續為USD/JPY 提供下檔支撐。二、被視為鴿派的日
本經濟財政大臣城內實明確表態，貨幣政策應交由日本央行自行判斷，並宣告日本
已擺脫長期通縮、轉型為增長導向經濟體。此番言論降低政治干預日銀進一步升息
的疑慮，在貨幣政策正常化的腳步下，預計將限縮 USD/JPY 的上行空間。三、就技
術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY 匯價目前交投於月線及年線之間，各天期均線高度
糾結，多空雙方於 158 關卡附近拉鋸，在有效突破季線及年線反壓或跌破月線支撐
前 ， 仍以區間震盪看待。 綜合以上 ， 預估USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高 。

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 EUR/USD 震盪走升。週初，持續受法國財政赤字與歐債利差擔憂拖累，
EUR/USD 震盪走弱；週間，因美國9 月 CPI 高於預期，市場重新評估聯準會年底前
升息腳步，導致 EUR/USD 跳水最低來到 1.1112；臨近週末，儘管美國 PPI 與零售
銷售數據偏熱，市場優先反映美債殖利率回落與風險資產重新定價，美元多頭獲利
了結帶動 EUR/USD 谷底反彈 ， 最高來到1.1269， 終場收在1.1239， 週線上漲42 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 區間偏空震盪，主要原因如下：一、法國央行月度展望報
告指出，法國第三季 GDP 預估僅微幅成長 0.1%，印證近期高能源的成本已實質拖
累歐元區核心成員國增長。同時，歐洲央行總裁拉加德明確拒絕出手干預法債，要
求法國透過自身財政政策化解赤字困境；在明年法國大選前的財政隱憂與基本面疲
弱下，預計將令歐元上檔持續承壓。二、儘管歐洲央行管委溫施與總裁拉加德保留
12 月升息預期，加上德國基建與軍費支出為歐元提供部分下檔保護，但市場穩固預
期聯準會年底前將再升息一次。在美國經濟韌性與高利差優勢主導下，預計將限縮
歐元反彈空間。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 自低點反彈並站回五日線
之上，然而中長期均線下彎仍維持空頭排列，RSI 指標亦處於 50 中軸下方，顯示整
體空方趨勢尚未扭轉，反彈至月線附近恐面臨較大賣壓。綜合以上，預估EUR/USD
本週呈現區間偏空震盪的可能性較高。

                           新台幣利率走勢分析

115. 10.12-115.10.16 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.830%  
30 天期短票利率   1.67% ~ 1.68% 
公債－10 年期-A15109     2.020~2.025% 
－ 5 年期- A15108 1.88%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分 ，本週央行連續減發定存單使市場資金寬鬆 ， 雖外資大舉匯
出，資金供給仍充裕，帶動短率朝區間低位移動。 
週五央行標售 2 年期定存單 250 億元 ， 加權平均得標利率為1.58%， 低於市場
預期區間 1.60%-1.62%，較上期標售結果下跌 5.8 個基本點。 
週 五 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.830% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.67%~1.68%，利率大致持平於上週。 
二、 5 年期指標券 A15108 週二搓合成交在 1.88%，後隨美債利率滑落，加上本週
2 年期公債標債結果提振市場持券信心，帶動 10 年期指標券 A15109 週四向
下成交在 2.02%~2.025%。 
在銀行去化短期資金及補充 LCR 之需求下 ， 週三2 年期公債得標利率為1.65%，
落在市場預估區間下緣 ， 投標倍數回升至2.86 倍 ， 銀行業得標比重上升至88%。
股市承壓帶動避險需求，美債利率大幅回檔，促使台債交投偏空氛圍轉趨和
緩 ， 惟在央行維持緊縮貨幣政策下 ， 買盤積極追價的意願不強 ， 預期短線台債
殖利率呈區間格局的可能性較高，關注下週 10 年券標售結果，預估 5 年券區
間為 1.85%~1.90%，10 年券區間為 1.98%~2.03%。

关注 PPI 超预期与CPI 低迷背后的价格传导困局            
      ——2026 年10 月价格数据点评

事件：2026 年10 月全国居民消费价格指数（CPI）同比0.7%（前
值 0.9%），环比 0%（前值 0.2%），1-10 月 CPI 同比 0.9%（前值
0.9%）；核心 CPI 同比 0.8%（前值 1%），环比 0%（前值 0%），1-10
月核心 CPI 同比 1.1%（前值 1.1%）；工业生产者出厂价格指数（PPI）
同比 5.7%（前值 5%），环比 0.7%（前值 1.2%），1-10 月 PPI 同比
2.7%（前值2.3%）。 
食品价格走弱、工业品价格支撑减弱，CPI 环比由正转平,略高于
季节性水平，CPI 同比涨幅边际回落；下半年以来，CPI 累计同比及核
心 CPI 累计同比分别维持在 0.9%和 1.1%，较上半年均回落 0.1 个百分
点，价格修复节奏放缓。10 月 CPI 同比增长 0.7%，较上月回落 0.2 个
百分点，结束连续两个月回升走势，环比 由上月上涨 0.2%转为持平，
强于季节性水平（2023 年-2025 年，10 月CPI 环比均值为-0.1%）。10
月核心 CPI（剔除食品和能源）同比增长 0.8%，较上月回落 0.2 个百
分点，环比 0%，低于季节性水平（2023 年-2025 年，10 月核心 CPI 环
比均值为 0.1%）。1-10 月，CPI 同比及核心 CPI 同比分别为 0.9%和
1.1%，与前三季度持平，较上年底回升，但均较上半年回落 0.1 个百
分点，终端消费需求持续修复但仍偏弱。 
 食品价格 环比由涨转降 ，且低于季节性水平 ，同比降幅 连续两个月
扩大，食品价格偏弱是本月 CPI 的主要拖累。10 月食品价格同比下
降 2.9%，降幅比上月扩大 1.2 个百分点，连续两个月降幅扩大，影
响CPI 同比下降约0.49 个百分，环比由上月上涨0.4%转为下降1.0%，
低于季节性水平（ 2023 年-2025 年，10 月食品价格 环比 均值为-
0.6%），影响 CPI 环比下降约 0.17 个百分点。其中，气候适宜、夏
秋蔬菜供应衔接顺畅 ，蛋类、鲜菜和鲜果价格 环比均由正转负， 10
月环比分别下降 5.2%、5.8%和 0.6%；市场供应充足下，水产品价格
环比降幅扩大、猪肉价格环比由正转负。

关注PPI 超预期与CPI 低迷背后的价格传导困局 
 工业消费品价格支撑 CPI 环比上行但支撑作用有所减弱。10 月工业消费品价格环比上涨 0.3%，
影响CPI 环比上涨约0.10 个百分点（9 月工业消费品影响CPI 环比上涨约0.22 个百分点），
工业消费品对 CPI 的支撑作用减弱。工业消费品中，国内汽油价格上涨 4.2%，影响 CPI 环比
上涨约0.14 个百分点，油价仍是CPI 的重要支撑。受国际金价下行影响，10 月黄金饰品价格
环比下降5.9%，环比连续两个月为负，影响10 月CPI 环比下降约0.03 个百分点,是CPI 环比
修复的重要拖累。 
 假期效应带动下， 服务价格环比降转涨，高于季节性水平，其中宾馆住宿、飞机票等假期相
关消费价格环比均由负转正。10 月，服务价格同比上涨 0.7%，涨幅较上月回落 0.1 个百分点，
或主要受中秋错期影响（今年中秋节在9 月，去年在10 月），服务价格环比由上月下降0.2%
转为上涨0.1%，对CPI 的影响由上月的拖累转为支持，影响CPI 环比上涨约0.04 个百分点，
高于季节性（2023-2025 年 10 月，服务价格环比均值为 0.03%）。具体来看，国庆假期出行
人数增加，宾馆住宿、飞机票和旅行社收费环比均由负转正，10 月份分别上涨 6.5%、3.3%和
2.4%，合计影响CPI 环比上涨约0.04 个百分点。

关注PPI 超预期与CPI 低迷背后的价格传导困局 
PPI 环比连续三个月正增，10 月 PPI 同比 5.7%、为 2022 年 7 月以来高点。1-10 月 PPI
同比 2.7%，较前值上行 0.4 个百分点，为 2023 年以来的高点。10 月 PPI 同比 5.7%，较上月
上行 0.7 个百分点，PPI 环比 0.7%，连续三个月维持正增。PPI 与 CPI 剪刀差进一步扩大至 5
个百分点，价格传导仍存在阻碍。PPI 分项中，生产资料价格同比与生活资料价格同比延续一
正一负，二者剪刀差边际扩大。其中，生产资料同比7.4%，较前值上行0.9 个百分点，是PPI
同比上行的重要支撑，生活资料同比-0.1%，降幅与上月相同，二者剪刀差较上月扩大 0.9 个
百分点，终端需求偏弱，上下游行业分化加剧。当前 PPI 上涨仍主要集中在资源品行业和部
分高技术行业，下游企业的成本承受能力需重点关注。结合 PPI 与 PPIRM 剪刀差来看，今年
以来，二者剪刀差由平转负，且差值波动扩大，10 月二者剪刀差较前值扩大 0.3 个百分点，
二者分别为 5.7%和 7.9%，PPIRM 上涨的速度显著高于 PPI，企业尤其是中下游企业盈利承压
较大。

### Page 4

3 
www.ccxi.com.cn 
2026 年10 月价格数据点评  
关注PPI 超预期与CPI 低迷背后的价格传导困局 
 生产资料价格环比连续 3 个月维持正增，输入性因素、季节性因素、反内卷深入推进以及人
工智能行业快速发展带动部分相关行业价格上行，是 PPI 环比正增、同比上行的重要支撑。
具体来看，一是 10 月国际油价整体维持高位震荡，中东地缘风险溢价持续支撑油价。受国际
油价波动影响，石油和天然气开采及石油、煤炭及其他燃料加工业价格环比连续 3 个月为正，
10 月分别为 6%和 6.1%，化学原料及化学制品制造业价格环比连续两个月保持正增，10 月为
3.1%，均显著高于全部工业品 PPI 环比增速水平。二是受需求共振与能源冲击影响，煤炭加
工、燃气生产和供应业价格环比均为正，分别为 3.2%和 1.5%，对 PPI 环比改善带来一定支撑。
三是产业治理深入推进， 推动部分行业供求关系持续改善、价格水平持续修复，其中 非金属
矿物制品业（包括水泥、玻璃等）价格环比连续两个月正增，10 月环比上涨 0.8%，汽车制造
业价格环比在连续下降 3 个月后于 10 月份转为持平。四是人工智能持续发展，对 AI 产业链
相关行业价格上行带来助力，其中 电气机械和器材制造业、计算机通信和其他电子设备制造
业价格环比均上涨0.1%，亦对PPI 上行带来支撑。  
 生活资料价格环比由正转负，同比仍为负，终端消费仍偏弱。10 月生活资料价格同比降幅与
9 月相同，均为-0.1%，环比由正转负，显示当前生活资料价格仍偏弱。从分项来看，衣着类、
一般日用品类价格环比均由平转负，食品类价格环比由正转平，耐用消费品类价格环比由正
转负。结合 CPI 分项中耐用消费品价格来看，以家电为主的家用器具价格环比连续 3 个月为
负且降幅逐月扩大，显示以旧换新政策对耐用消费品价格支撑作用持续减弱。

### Page 5

4 
www.ccxi.com.cn 
2026 年10 月价格数据点评  
关注PPI 超预期与CPI 低迷背后的价格传导困局

整体来看， 10 月物价走势延续分化， 受输入性和季节性因素带动主要能源价格上涨等
多因素影响，PPI 同比涨幅创2022 年下半年以来新高，显著高于此前市场预期的5.3%左右，
其中石油和天然气开采业、煤炭开采和洗选业、精炼石油产品制造、有机化学原料制造、煤
炭加工、燃气生产和供应业等能源相关 6 个行业合计影响 PPI 环比上涨约 0.61 个百分点，
超过总涨幅的八成。但与此同时，CPI 同比涨幅回落、生活资料 PPI 同比和环比均为负，需
求偏弱下，上下游价格分化仍在加剧，价格传导困局仍存。后续来看，输入性因素仍是短期
影响物价走势的关键变量之一，11 月初，美国中期选举落定，民主党拿下参众两院，同时伊
朗释放和谈信号，地缘担忧有所回落，国际油价回落，截至11 月6 日，WTI 原油期货结算价
已有 10 月底的 104 美元/桶回落至 90.8 美元/桶，但仍位于较高水平。同时迎峰度冬带来煤、
气阶段性需求增加，PPI 同比短期维持较高水平仍有支撑。不过若后续国际能源价格涨幅延
续回落，叠加考虑去年四季度基数波动走高，PPI 上行斜率或边际放缓。CPI 方面，蔬菜随
降温进入季节性涨价窗口，但生猪供给仍偏充裕，猪价缺乏持续大涨基础，食品价格短期仍
偏弱。此外，长假期结束，服务价格短期面临回落压力，对 CPI 的支撑或减弱。整体来看，
短期 CPI 大概率维持低位温和运行，实现年初2%的目标存在较大压力。

## Document 102: 2026年・冬のボーナス予測 ～前年比＋3.0％と６年連続の増加を予想～

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年・冬のボーナス予測 ～前年比＋3.0％と６年連続の増加を予想～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-06
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Economic Trends

2026 年 11 月 6 日

1/2 
2026 年・冬のボーナス予測 
～前年比＋3.0％と６年連続の増加を予想～ 
 経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト  新家 義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

○ 民間企業の2026 年冬のボーナス一人当たり支給額を前年比＋3.0％（昨冬同＋2.8％）と予想する
（厚生労働省「毎月勤労統計」ベース）。冬のボーナスとしては６年連続の増加となるだろう。中
東情勢の悪化に伴う資源価格上昇が企業活動の重荷となるなかでも企業収益は高水準を維持してい
る。人手不足を背景とした処遇改善の動きも根強く、今冬もボーナスの増加が続くと予想する。

○ 11 月６日に厚生労働省から公表された26 年夏のボーナス（賞与支給事業所における労働者一人平
均賞与額）は、前年比＋5.3％と５年連続で増加し、昨夏の同＋2.9％から伸びも拡大した。また、
賞与を支給していない事業所も含む「全事業所における労働者一人平均賞与額」で見ても同＋
5.9％と増加している。賞与を支給する事業所の割合も前年の73.4％から74.0％へ上昇しており、
企業の従業員への還元姿勢が強いことがうかがえる。

○ ボーナスの交渉では、春闘時にその年の年間賞与を決定する夏冬型が採用されていることが多い。
2026 年春闘では、月例給与だけでなく一時金でも増額回答が目立った。企業収益が高水準にある
ことに加え、物価高への配慮や人材確保の観点から、賞与を含めた処遇改善を進める企業が多かっ
たとみられる。また、月例給与の引き上げが賞与の算定基礎額を押し上げ、支給月数の増加と相ま
ってボーナス増につながる面もある。夏のボーナス増に続き、冬についてもこうした交渉結果が反
映される形で増加が予想される。

○ 毎期型の企業、あるいは労働組合が存在しない中小・零細企業においては、より直近の収益状況・
業況がボーナスに反映されやすい。もっとも、イラン情勢悪化によるコスト上昇が企業収益の下押
し要因となるなかでも、価格転嫁の進展などを背景として企業収益は総じて底堅く推移している。
利益の水準も高く、従業員への還元余力はなお保たれているとみてよいだろう。

○ 人手不足感が強いことも、ボーナス増加を後押しする。企業にとって人材の確保・定着は引き続き
重要な経営課題となっており、月例給与だけでなく賞与を含めた処遇改善の必要性は高い。物価高
によって家計負担が増加するなか、従業員への還元を求める声も強い。こうした点も今冬のボーナ
ス増加を支える要因となる。

○ 労務行政研究所が９月30 日に公表した調査によれば、東証プライム上場企業175 社の2026 年年末

### Page 2

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
2/2 
一時金妥結額は、前年比＋4.2％と５年連続で増加した。製造業が同＋4.3％、非製造業が同＋
3.5％と、いずれもプラスとなっている。大企業中心の調査であり、中小企業やパートタイム労働
者も含む毎月勤労統計とは対象が異なるものの、冬のボーナスも堅調に増加するという見方を裏付
ける結果といえる。

○ 冬のボーナス増加は家計の所得環境にとって好材料であり、個人消費を下支えすることが期待され
る。一方で懸念されるのが物価上昇の影響である。中東情勢悪化に伴うコスト上昇の価格転嫁など
を背景として、今後は物価上昇率が高まる可能性が高く、政府による電気・ガス代支援の効果を織
り込んでも、2027 年初にはＣＰＩコアの前年比上昇率が＋３％台に達することが予想される。物
価上昇による家計の購買力の圧迫が続くなか、ボーナス増加による個人消費の押し上げ効果につい
ては過度な期待は禁物だろう。

## Document 142: 261105_daiwa_nypcb

Source PDF: all report pdf/261105_daiwa_nypcb.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Summary: NYPCB released its 3Q26 results after market hours on 5 November. Net profit 
was TWD3.30bn (EPS: TWD5.10), 9% below our estimate and the Bloomberg consensus. 
Revenue rose 21% QoQ and 49% YoY to TWD16.38bn, in line with expectations. GM was 
29.3%, 1.0ppt below our estimate and 0.7ppt below consensus, reflecting yield losses 
during the Kunshan ramp-up and a more modest BT substrate margin uplift than we had 
assumed. OPM was 26.4%, versus our 27.5% estimate and 26.5% consensus. Despite 
operating profit being broadly in line, net profit was weighed down by a higher tax rate 
(26% vs. our 22% estimate), reflecting increased profitability at NYPCB’s China factories.

Looking ahead, we expect modest BT and c.10% ABF price hikes in 4Q26, which would 
result in mid-teens % QoQ growth in 4Q26E. This is consistent with its October revenue of 
TWD6.29bn (released 2 November, up 13% MoM and 70% YoY), equivalent to 34% of our 
4Q26 forecast (historic range being 33-39%). Per management, the Kunshan plant’s UTR 
has increased to 90-95% in October (vs. 80-85% in 3Q26 and 65-70% in 2Q26). As 
NYPCB is unwilling to accept LTAs with end clients (compared with its competitors), we 
believe its bargaining power should be the highest among TW substrate vendors. We have 
a Buy (1) rating on NYPCB, with a 12-month TP of TWD2,460, based on a target PER of 
49x applied to our 1-year forward EPS forecast. Key downside risk: weaker-than-expected 
demand for switches.

What’s the impact: 
Below is our first take on NYPCB’s 3Q26 results, released on 5 November.

• 3Q26 net profit missed expectations. Net profit came in at TWD3.30bn (EPS: 
TWD5.10), 9% below both our estimate (TWD3.63bn) and the Bloomberg consensus 
(TWD3.62bn). 3Q26 revenue of TWD16.38bn (up 21% QoQ and 49% YoY) was in-line 
with expectations, while the gross margin reached 29.3%, 1ppt/0.7ppt below 
our/Bloomberg consensus estimates due to loss on the yield rate as the Kunshan plant 
ramped up; our gross margin expansion assumption for BT substrate was also too 
bullish. Operating margin reached 26.4%, versus our 27.5% estimate and the street’s 
26.5%, mainly on the lower-than-expected gross margin. However, while the operating 
profit was largely on track with expectations, net profit was dragged down by a higher-
than-expected tax rate (26% vs. our 22% estimate) due to rising profitability from 
factories in China.

## Document 165: 261108_citi_MSI

Source PDF: all report pdf/261108_citi_MSI.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-08
Pages: 14
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Micro-Star (2377.TW)
3Q26 Preview: Margins to Stay Elevated on Cost Pass-Through; Raise 
TP to NT$140, Stay Neutral
CITI'S TAKE 
MSI will report 3Q26 results in mid-Nov. We now expect net profit of 
NT$3.19bn and EPS of NT$3.77, 18% above con estimates, as 3Q26 GPM 
stays elevated at 15.8% vs. our previous 12.0% estimates, supported by 
better VGA supply and continued ASP pass-through of rising memory 
costs. For 4Q26E, we expect MB/VGA shipments to ease QoQ but ASPs to 
rise, with server contribution to further increase. Looking into 2027E, we 
expect PC demand to remain weak and pass-through room to narrow as 
memory price increases pace slows down. We raise 2026E-28E earnings by 
10-57% and lift our TP to NT$140 (from NT$118; 12x 2027E EPS). We stay 
Neutral, with likely limited upside given uncertain PC recovery and modest 
server contribution. 
3Q26 preview — MSI reported 3Q26 revenue of NT$56.9bn (+14% QoQ/-1% YoY), 
broadly in line with Citi’s forecast and slightly ahead of cons. While Sep MB and VGA 
shipments both exceeded expectations, as VGA supply improved. With memory and 
other raw material prices continuing to rise and MSI able to pass costs through, we 
expect 3Q26 GPM to stay elevated at 15.8%, well above our previous 12.0% estimate. 
Assuming only a modest increase in opex ratio, we forecast net profit of NT$3.19bn 
(+6% QoQ/+88% YoY) and EPS of NT$3.77, 18% above cons forecasts.
Price hike continues — We expect 4Q26E shipments to decline from 3Q26’s peak 
season. Mgmt. also indicated that Oct shipment could go down from Sep high base. 
However, we raise our 4Q26E pricing expectations and believe ASP should stay 
elevated, as memory prices keep climbing and MSI continues to pass costs through. 
The long-cultivated server business should also contribute higher than previous 
quarters. Looking into 2027, PC demand should remain weak as component costs 
stay high; we expect MSI’s MB/VGA/PC shipments to decline by double digits, with 
pass-through room narrowing as the price hike pace slows down.
Implications — We raise our 2026E-28E earnings forecasts by 10-57%, reflecting 
sustained strong component cost pass-through. We lift our TP to NT$140 (from 
NT$118), implying 12x 2027E EPS (rolled over from 12x 2026E EPS). We believe the 
share price already reflects MSI’s ability to pass costs through to end products, and 
we see limited upside, given that the PC demand recovery remains highly uncertain 
and the contribution from the new server business is still limited. Stay Neutral.

### Page 6

Micro-Star (2377.TW)

Micro-Star
Company description
Established in 1986 and listed in 1998, Taiwan-based MSI is a leading
OBM/ODM for gaming PCs, motherboards, graphic cards, industrial PCs, and
servers. MSI started business as a motherboard manufacturer but
transformed itself to become a leading gaming PC play in 2012 with a
comprehensive product portfolio across the gaming system and components.
MSI enjoys about 16% global share in the gaming NB industry, and is ranked

No.3 in graphic card shipments and No.3 in motherboard shipments globally.
Investment strategy
We rate MSI shares at Neutral as we anticipate earnings upward pressure
from the delince in PC and MB/VGA shipments in the near-term. While the
soaring component costs could be partially offset by price hike, we expect
muted demand and AI resource crowding to wiegh on margins upside. Further
upside maintains limited, though, until we see a meaningful recovery in core

gaming demand or a more substantial contribution from the server business.
Valuation
Our target price of NT$140 is based on 12x 2027E EPS, below the stock's five-
year average forward 12-month PE. This valuation reflects the earnings
downside risks from muted PC demand and margin pressure from soaring
component costs, while accounting for the time required for the server
business to achieve meaningful scale.
Risks
Key upside risks that could cause MSI's shares to trade above our target price

include: 1) a sharper-than-expected recovery in high-end gaming demand;
and 2) faster-than-anticipated revenue contribution from the AI server
segment. Key downside risks include: 1) a deeper-than-expected contraction
in global PC shipments; 2) prolonged supply shortages for gaming GPUs due
to Ai resource crowding; and 3) inability to pass through surging memory and
CPU costs to price-sensitive consumers.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

## Document 175: 261108_ms_wtmicro

### Page 1

M
Update
Broader recovery supports record earnings. 3Q revenue was NT$597.3bn (+1% Q/
Q, +82% Y/Y), with gross margin of 3.6% (+0.2ppt Q/Q, -0.1ppt Y/Y, 0.3ppt above
MSe), and net profit of NT$10.3bn (+6% Q/Q, +170% Y/Y, 42% above MSe). Data-
center revenue fell 5% Q/Q on shipment timing, while industrial, automotive and
Future Electronics revenue increased sequentially. The richer mix lifted gross margin
19bp Q/Q to 3.60%; operating margin rose 18bp to 2.38%, with operating expenses

increasing only 2% Q/Q. Net financing costs rose 22% Q/Q to NT$1.94bn.
AI drives a stronger 4Q. Management guides 4Q revenue of NT$730-770bn, with
the NT$750bn midpoint up 26% Q/Q, as data-center and communications growth
outweigh seasonal declines across consumer applications, industrial and automotive.
The guided gross-margin midpoint falls 36bp Q/Q to 3.24% on mix, but operating
profit rises 19% Q/Q to NT$16.96bn as expenses grow only 1%. 4Q EPS guidance is

NT$8.07–9.05, with the NT$8.56 midpoint up 7% Q/Q. Financing costs of NT$2.6–
2.8bn and the larger weighted-average share base temper the translation from
operating-profit growth into EPS.
Growth broadens, but cash conversion matters. Future Electronics grew over 40%
Y/Y in 3Q and has started shipping data-center products. Management remains
positive on 2027 AI demand, without providing a full-year earnings forecast.
Inventory days increased Q/Q from 47 to 66  and net debt from NT$27.6bn to NT

$46.4bn, although longer payables kept the cash-conversion cycle near 19 days
versus 18 in 2Q. We see further earnings potential from AI scale and Future’s
recovery, with funding requirements and inventory conversion the key execution
risks.
What’s Changed

shipments accelerate, supporting further profit growth despite a
less favorable mix. The key earnings sensitivity is how much
operating leverage offsets rising funding costs.

### Page 4

M
Update
4
Valuation methodology
We cut our price target by 15% to  NT$296: We update our residual income model to
reflect earnings estimate revisions. We increase our equity risk premium assumption from
11.8% to 12.5% to reflect segment volatilities, and reduce our intermediate growth rate
assumption from 10% to 8% to reflect a more conservative medium-term growth forecast.
We keep other key RI assumptions unchanged: 2.0% risk-free rate, 0.9 beta, cash dividend

payout ratio of 85%, and a long-term growth rate of 3.0%. Bull case PT is cut from NT
$406 to NT$344.5, implying 10.6x 2027 P/E. Bear case PT is cut from NT$230 to NT$160,
implying 4.9x 2027 P/E. The risk reward is now skewed more towards the bear case due to
potential downstream demand disruption.
Exhibit 3: WT: Residual Income Model
Source: Morgan Stanley Research estimates

Relative  valuation

forward P/E since 2018, which we see as fair given improving demand and the company's
continued progress in penetrating the AI supply chain.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

[Nhận định xu hướng] 
VN-Index có thể hồi kỹ thuật từ cạnh dưới tam giác, song việc chớm thủng hỗ trợ

Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -29.18 điểm tương ứng -1.68%, với thanh khoản suy 
giảm. Dầu khí, Bảo hiểm và Dịch vụ phân phối là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất 
trong tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -3,013 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã MCH, VIC và FPT; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều mua ròng ở mức 
887 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều bán ròng -2,021 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua và đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -4,384 hợp đồng. Tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng -
4,275 hợp đồng và đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -7,815 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam tiếp tục điều chỉnh tuần thứ tư liên tiếp, khi VN-Index lùi về đóng cửa 1.706 
điểm - giảm khoảng 1,7% so với tuần trước và đang dao động quanh vùng hỗ trợ tâm lý 
1.700 điểm, cũng là vùng đáy cũ của tháng 8. Thanh khoản thị trường tiếp tục suy yếu, 
phản ánh tâm lý thận trọng cao độ trong bối cảnh thị trường thiếu vắng thông tin hỗ trợ 
trong nước, trong khi các yếu tố bên ngoài như xung đột tại Iran, và chi phí vốn toàn cầu 
chưa hạ nhiệt tiếp tục đè nặng tâm lý nhà đầu tư. Khối ngoại duy trì chuỗi bán ròng kéo 
dài nhiều phiên liên tiếp, nối dài đà rút vốn đã lên tới xấp xỉ 95 nghìn tỷ đồng từ đầu năm. 
Trong một, hai tuần tới, mùa báo cáo kết quả kinh doanh quý 3 được kỳ vọng là chất xúc 
tác giúp thị trường giao dịch cân bằng trở lại.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX tiếp tục hình thành mẫu nến giảm điểm tiêu cực trên đồ thị tuần, đánh dấu tuần 
giảm thứ 4 liên tiếp, cho thấy áp lực phân phối vẫn đang có phần lấn át. Trên khung ngày, 
tuy chỉ số đã có nỗ lực phục hồi trở lại trong 3 phiên đan xen, đặc biệt là phản ứng trong 
phiên cuối tuần khi chạm trendline trung hạn, nhưng việc chỉ có thể hình thành các mẫu 
nến "spinning" hàm ý đà tích lũy chưa thực sự thuyết phục. Trong một vài phiên kế tiếp, 
VNINDEX có thể xuất hiện nhịp hồi kỹ thuật từ vùng hỗ trợ, tương ứng với cạnh dưới tam 
giác, nhưng sau khi chớm phá vùng hỗ trợ quanh 1710 và hiện chỉ còn một số ít các mã giữ 
được xu hướng tăng tính từ đáy tháng 7, xu hướng ngắn hạn đang trở nên khá trung tính.

(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên) 
Kháng cự xa: 1780 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1740 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1680 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1630 (+-15)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

### Page 1

CPI 公布后，交易员开始预计 12 ⽉有较⼩可能加息 50 个基点；美联储今年票委洛根称，今
冬可能需要更快加息，联储⾄少还需两次 25 基点加息再暂停。此后，美债收益率升幅进⼀
步扩⼤， 10 年期收益率重上 5.0% 、⽇内升超 10 个基点；现货⻩⾦⼀度跌超 3% 、现货⽩银
曾跌超 4% 。
摘要：
美国 10 ⽉通胀再度超出市场预期，美债遭遇抛售，收益率全线⼤幅攀升；美元⾛强、利率上⾏
预期升温，也令⻩⾦和⽩银承压。美联储官员接连释放鹰派信号，市场不仅进⼀步押注 12 ⽉加
息，也开始评估更⼤幅度加息以及明年进⼀步收紧货币政策的可能性。
美东时间 10 ⽇周⼆，美国 CPI 公布后，美国国债遭集中抛售，美债价格跳⽔、收益率加速上⾏。
基准 10 年期美债收益率在 CPI 公布后升幅扩⼤，美联储官员洛根（ Lorie Logan ）讲话后，美股
午盘时段⼀度升破 5.01% ，⽇内升超 10 个基点，对美联储政策利率预期更敏感的 2 年期美债收益
率升幅更⼤，美股午盘曾升破 4.77% ，⽇内升超 20 个基点，势将创去年 4 ⽉以来最⼤单⽇升幅。

贵⾦属市场同步承压。美国 CPI 公布后，⻩⾦盘中跌幅扩⼤。美股午盘时段，纽约⻩⾦期货刷新
⽇低⾄ 4020.8 美元 / 盎司，⽇内跌近 2.3% ；现货⻩⾦曾跌⾄ 4014 美元 / 盎司下⽅，⽇内跌近
3.1% 。⽩银跌势更为明显，⾏情显⽰，美股午盘刷新⽇低时，纽约期银⽇内跌近 3% ，现货⽩银
⽇内跌超 4% 。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

CPI 通胀连⽉超预期，债市重新定价美联储政策路径
美国劳⼯统计局周⼆公布的数据显⽰， 10 ⽉ CPI 环⽐上涨 0.4% ，⾼于市场预期的 0.3% ，但较 9 ⽉
的 0.6% 有所放缓；剔除⻝品和能源价格的核⼼ CPI 环⽐上涨 0.3% ，同样⾼于市场预期的 0.2% ，
与 9 ⽉涨幅持平。这也是核⼼ CPI 连续第三个⽉上涨 0.3% 。由于去年同期美国联邦政府停摆导致
相关数据⽆法采集，劳⼯统计局此次没有公布 10 ⽉ CPI 同⽐数据。
CPI 数据公布⼏⼩时前，市场已经受到美联储理事沃勒（ Christopher Waller ）的鹰派⾔论冲
击。
沃勒本周⼆在伦敦发表讲话时表⽰，即使 10 ⽉通胀有所改善，他仍倾向于⽀持美联储 12 ⽉加息
25 个基点；但如果核⼼ CPI 连续第三个⽉达到 0.3% 或更⾼，就应考虑在 12 ⽉加息 50 个基点。他
警告称，只加息 25 个基点可能损害美联储抗通胀的公信⼒。
实际公布的核⼼ CPI 恰好达到沃勒设定的 0.3% ⻔槛。数据公布后，市场迅速提⾼对更⼤幅度加

息的押注。路透报道称，交易员对美联储 12 ⽉加息 50 个基点的隐含概率⼀度升⾄约 13.6% ，⽽
数据公布前这⼀概率接近于零；相⽐之下， 25 个基点加息已基本成为市场主流预期。
彭博报道称，通胀数据推动美债遭遇抛售，短期限美债承受的压⼒尤其明显。货币市场也开始
加⼤对美联储到 2027 年年中⾄少再加息两次的押注，并更明显地计⼊ 12 ⽉⼀次性加息 50 个基点
的可能性。
Principal Asset Management ⾸席全球策略师 Seema Shah 表⽰： “ 周⼆的通胀报告是⼀记警
告。它向美联储传递的信息令⼈不安但⾮常明确，那就是和通胀的战⽃还没有结束。 ”
⾼盛资产管理多部⻔固定收益投资主管 Lindsay Rosner 表⽰，如果 CPI 持续强劲，美联储可能推
动更激进的紧缩周期。她的观点反映出市场对通胀持续⾼企的担忧：即便 12 ⽉加息已经成为基
准预期，后续政策路径仍可能⽐此前预想的更加鹰派。
今年票委洛根：今冬或需更快加息，联储⾄少还需两次 25 基点加息再暂停

CPI 公布后，达拉斯联储主席洛根也释放了进⼀步收紧货币政策的信号。洛根是今年拥有 FOMC
会议投票权的票委。她周⼆在达拉斯联储和堪萨斯城联储联合举办的能源会议上表⽰，按照当
前经济和通胀前景，美联储⾄少还需要实施两次 25 个基点的加息。

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
8 收藏 分享到 :
写评论
图⽚
洛根指出，具体加息节奏取决于短期经济和⾦融环境，今年冬季美联储有可能⽐今年秋季采取
更快的加息步伐。她表⽰： “ 今年冬季可能有必要⽐ FOMC 今年秋季迄今采取的步伐更快地⾏
动。 ”
不过，洛根并⾮主张持续不断地加息。她表⽰，在完成上述两次加息后，美联储可以暂停⾏
动，评估经济和⾦融市场的发展，再决定是否需要进⼀步调整政策。她还强调，通胀⻓期⾼于
⽬标，可能逐渐嵌⼊企业和家庭的⾏为预期，因此及时采取⾏动⼗分重要。
洛根的表态意味着，部分美联储官员正在讨论的不仅是 12 ⽉是否加息，还包括如何更快地将利
率推向温和限制性⽔平。她与沃勒的讲话前后呼应，令市场对美联储未来数⽉进⼀步收紧政策
的担忧升温。
美债收益率和美元⻬升，⾦银遭双重压⼒
美债收益率和美元同步⾛强，给不⽣息的贵⾦属带来压⼒。⼀⽅⾯，债券收益率上升提⾼了持

有⻩⾦、⽩银等⾮⽣息资产的机会成本；另⼀⽅⾯，美元⾛强也令以美元计价的贵⾦属对其他
货币持有者更加昂贵。
路透报道称， 10 ⽉通胀数据⾼于预期后，⻩⾦价格明显下跌，市场开始评估美联储采取更激进
加息⾏动的可能性。⻩⾦对利率预期变化较为敏感，若市场进⼀步上调对未来政策利率的预
期，贵⾦属短期仍可能⾯临压⼒。
不过，⻩⾦和⽩银的后续⾛势并不只取决于美联储政策。美元⾛势、地缘政治⻛险、能源价格
以及投资者对经济前景的判断，都可能影响贵⾦属需求。当前市场的关键变化在于，通胀数据
和美联储官员的鹰派表态重新强化了利率上⾏预期，使此前受益于降息预期的贵⾦属交易⾯临
调整。

## Document 420: NZ Q3 Labour market data - Potential RBNZ implications (2026-11-11)

Source PDF: all report pdf/NZ Q3 Labour market data - Potential RBNZ implications (2026-11-11).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-11
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#labour #newzealand
NZ Q3 Labour market data - Potential RBNZ implications
By Kelly Eckhold
Chief Economist, Westpac New Zealand
13:16 November 04 2026 Share
The Q3 labour market statistics were weaker than expected. We discuss potential
implications for the RBNZ noting that a December hike remains more likely than not at this
point.
Q3 Labour market data: potential RBNZ implications
The Q3 labour market data were weaker than RBNZ and our expectations.

### Page 2

Today's labour market data seem unequivocally weaker than expected by either us or the RBNZ. While
likely not a gamechanger for the cycle overall, it will add ammunition to the arguments of the MPC
doves that a pause in interest rate rises will make sense soon - perhaps in December or in the New
Year. It’s likely the case that a bit much was priced into December, so the change in market
probabilities in the wake of today’s data makes sense - although my base case remains that a
December rate rise remains more likely than not.
The message from the RBNZ in the October meeting though was to be careful about pricing in "done
deals" and that the outlook will be data-dependent. As we noted at the time, alongside measures of
inflation expectations, the key domestic data to watch ahead of the RBNZ’s 9 December meeting will
be indicators of what is happening to excess capacity (the output gap) as this will be a key determinant
of the Bank’s assessment of the outlook for inflation over the coming year.
It’s useful to remember in the RBNZ’s forecasting framework that GDP figures are the main
determinant of excess capacity in the economy (i.e., the output gap). The Q2 GDP data told us that the
output gap probably narrowed a bit through Q2, mainly reflecting historical revisions. In light of today’s
labour market numbers, the forecast for growth in Q3 may have some downside risk - but there are
conflicting indicators there, so it’s not that clear. However, it’s probably hard to argue that the level of
excess capacity reduced further in Q3 given today’s data. 
In the eyes of dovish MPC members, the rise in the unemployment rate and other measures important
for the RBNZ's labour market dashboard likely push in the direction of having greater comfort with the
medium-term inflation outlook. The vacancy to unemployment ratio increased a bit, labour constraints
eased a bit, and the unemployment rate has increased. Hence from a labour market perspective it
could be argued that policy could have more time all else equal. The outcome of the election, with the
possibility of a new RBNZ mandate, could increase the prominence of these factors - although this
won’t likely be an explicit factor in the RBNZ’s thinking in December even though it may be in the minds
of individual MPC members.
It could be the case that the argument for increases in the OCR track through 2027 could be
undermined with this data. It’s important to note (probably self-evidently) that there are other data that
will also be relevant. Hence the risk of a pause earlier in the New Year has probably increased with this
data, all else equal. I don't think our forecast peak OCR is really at issue here, but the time taken to get
there could be more flexible. Takes along the lines of "3% is the terminal rate" are very premature and
misplaced, in our view.

A December hike remains more likely than not, but there will be a clear debate to be had between the
MPC hawks and doves.
The forecast peak OCR is likely less impacted than the time to reach that peak. Further pauses in the
tightening cycle could come earlier than currently forecast. 
The outlook for the exchange rate is especially clouded by this data should it take a longer time for
NZ rates to move closer to global norms. 
Watch for updated RBNZ commentary on 10 and 19 November. The Governor’s observation that it
would have been useful to update markets ahead of the October meeting could imply a greater
willingness to comment in the coming weeks.

### Page 3

The outlook for the exchange rate is undermined by this data. I feel very happy about our year-end
target of 79c for the NZD/AUD rate with the risk that a recovery in the NZD takes longer than currently
assumed.
Looking ahead, there are some opportunities for the RBNZ to update markets on their views. Top of
mind is Paul Conway's 10 November speech to the UBS Australasia Conference. The Governor is also
speaking to the Institute of Directors on 19 November. If the RBNZ has something to say, then it might
happen in conjunction with these events (the 10 November speech is titled “How is the NZ economy
tracking”, so it will be difficult for Conway to avoid saying something about how he is interpreting
today’s data). We also note the Governor's comments in the October MPR that it could have been
useful to have been more flexible about updating markets on emerging views of new economic data.
This might increase the likelihood that these events could be used to provide guidance on the RBNZ’s
views of this data (and other emerging events), especially if it feels that the market’s interpretation is at
odds with its own.

Chief Economist

## Document 433: Rates Spark_ Back to a 5% 10Y UST on inflation fears

Source PDF: all report pdf/Rates Spark_ Back to a 5% 10Y UST on inflation fears.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-11
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

US Treasury bonds were quick to sell oﬀ as US CPI surprised to
the upside. We think EUR and GBP rates should be wary
of blindly following US inﬂation prints, as the economic
backdrop diﬀers signiﬁcantly. We challenge the market's
assumption that the ECB and Bank of England should
automatically follow a more hawkish Fed
Michiel Tukker
Impact from hot US inﬂation was ampliﬁed
by Fed comments
An unpleasant US inﬂation surprise for rates, taking the 10Y UST

yield right back to 5%, is a justiﬁed move in our view. A core CPI
reading of 0.3% MoM annualises to a yearly inﬂation number well
above 3% and far from the Fed’s target. Add additional uncertainty
from energy prices and AI investments, and markets are right to be
nervous. Last week’s solid payrolls report also gives little reason for
the Fed to choose a more dovish path for now.

### Page 2

Following on from Fed Governor Chris Waller's comment that he
backs a 25bp hike in December and could opt for a 50bp hike,
Treasury yields pushed even higher, with the 10yr up 6bp and 2yr
up 13bp since before Waller's comments. Markets are now more
than fully pricing in a 25bp hike in December, in line with our own
forecast, versus 20bp from before Waller's comments.
Nonetheless, the hopes of a Middle East de-escalation are pulling
energy costs lower, with retail gasoline prices now below $4/gallon

for the ﬁrst time since mid-July. This will certainly help give a
subdued November inﬂation print, but we won't get that until the
day after the December FOMC meeting. The Fed may get a sneak
peek, and that should give it the conﬁdence to only raise rates by
25bp. We are not forecasting any further hikes in 2027 as interest
rate-sensitive sectors cool further, with housing costs and lower
energy prices, amidst weak wage growth, dampening price
pressures in the economy.

European rates shouldn't follow hawkish US
repricing blindly
Euro and sterling rates quickly followed the Fed's hawkish repricing,
but we think those moves could fade fast. Interestingly, the 2Y EUR
inﬂation swap fell on the day, suggesting the spillovers were purely
in real terms. In other words, markets are quick to translate tighter
Fed policy into tighter ECB policy. But the reality is more complex.
Both regions face very diﬀerent inﬂation paths, whereby Europe is

more exposed to energy prices and the US to AI-related growth. A
tight correlation between US and European rates is warranted if
driven by oil as the common denominator, but less so if triggered
by macro data.
In addition, higher US rates imply tighter ﬁnancial conditions for
Europe, which means the ECB or Bank of England may not need to
act as aggressively as currently priced. Many ECB speakers have
indeed made the link between higher longer-dated interest rates

and the reduced need to increase policy rates. In other words,
markets are already doing the tightening. We therefore prefer to

## Document 440: September CPI_ Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas (2026-11-11)

Source PDF: all report pdf/September CPI_ Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas (2026-11-11).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-11
Pages: 18
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#inflation #australia #rba #economicbulletinsaustralia #inflation
September CPI: Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas
By Justin Smirk & Neha Sharma
16:08 October 28 2026 Share
September Monthly CPI: Headline 0.8%mth/4.3%yr, Trimmed Mean 0.3.%mth/3.6%yr.
Market services ex volatile 1.6%mth/4.0%yr. September Quarter CPI: Headline
1.3%qtr/3.9%yr; Trimmed Mean 1.0%qtr/3.7%yr.
The CPI rose 0.8% in the September month, stronger than Westpac’s 0.6% estimate and the

market’s expectation of 0.7%. The annual pace picked up from 4.0%yr to 4.3%yr, The seasonally
adjusted CPI rose 0.6% in September highlighting some positive seasonality in the month.

### Page 2

Key factors driving the headline monthly result were: automotive fuels (9.4%mth); domestic holiday
travel & accommodation (8.0%mth); international holiday travel & accommodation (7.2%mth); and
property rates & charges (5.5%mth). There were modest offsets from: women’s garments (–
3.1%mth); household textiles (–6.6%mth); and accessories (–4.1%mth).
The Trimmed Mean (TM) gained 0.3% in September, spot on both Westpac and market expectations
with the annual pace lifting from 3.5%yr (revised from 3.6%yr) to 3.6%yr. The six-month annualised

pace is now 4.0%yr the fastest in the short history of this series.
Headline CPI lifted 1.3% in the September quarter compared to an expectation of 1.3% by Westpac
and 1.2% by the market. This saw the annual pace hold at 3.9%yr.
The RBA’s key underlying inflation measure, the quarterly TM, lifted 1.0% vs an expectation of
0.9% by Westpac and market estimate of 1.0%. The annual pace lifted from 3.6%yr to 3.7%yr
with the six-month annualised pace accelerating to 3.7%yr from 3.4%yr.

Overall, the estimates come in broadly as expected so we see minimal risk to our current December
quarter forecast of 1.3%qtr for the headline CPI. Softer housing inflation does present a very modest
downside risk to our 0.9%qtr for the TM but there are offsetting gains elsewhere.
The September CPI update revealed underlying inflationary pressures continuing even while a
moderation in housing inflation (dwellings in particular) is unfolding slightly faster than we had

anticipated. As such, the near-term risk for inflation remains to the upside. The RBA will remain alert
to these near-term inflationary risks and as so, we hold to the view that the RBA will raise rates again
at the November meeting.

### Page 3

Month of September CPI
The monthly CPI rose 0.8%mth in September, above both our expectation of a 0.6%mth increase and
the market forecast of 0.7%mth. This lifted the annual pace by 0.3ppts to 4.3%yr. On a seasonally
adjusted basis, the CPI increased 0.6%mth.
The key contributors to the monthly increase were: automotive fuel (9.4%mth, +0.3ppts); and holiday
travel & accommodation (7.6%mth, +0.5ppts), alongside a smaller contribution from the annual reset in

property rates (5.5%mth, +0.1ppt). There was no single major offset. Instead, several components
across household furnishings and clothing & footwear recorded modest declines, together subtracting a
little over 0.1ppt from the monthly result.
The monthly trimmed mean (TM) rose 0.3%mth, in line with both our and the market’s expectations.
The annual pace edged up to 3.6%yr, although the August outcome was revised down to 3.5%yr.
Underlying inflation has remained broadly stuck in the 3.5–3.6%yr range since May. On a 3m/3m basis,

the monthly TM was 1.1%.

### Page 4

Market services excluding volatile items and holiday travel were flat in the month, with annual inflation
easing to 3.8%yr, its softest pace since November 2025. On a seasonally adjusted basis, monthly
inflation moderated across most components in this measure, with telecommunications services, rents
and other financial services the key exceptions. However, all but two components are still recording
annual price growth above 3%, suggesting that disinflation across market services remains slow and

relatively narrow. The persistence of these underlying price pressures is likely to remain a concern for
the RBA.

### Page 5

Housing inflation was notably softer in the month, rising 0.3%mth to be 5.2% higher over the year.
Rents increased 0.2%mth, slightly below our expectation for a 0.3%mth rise. The biannual indexation
of Commonwealth Rent Assistance occurred late in the month, weighing on the September outcome,
with the remainder of the impact expected to flow through in October. The ABS noted that rents would
have risen 0.3%mth in the absence of the measure, while on a seasonally adjusted basis rents

increased 0.4%mth.
New dwelling inflation was flat in the month, the softest reading since May 2025. Monthly inflation has
now slowed in each successive month since May 2026, suggesting homebuilders’ pricing power is
becoming increasingly constrained. The ABS noted that some project home builders raised base prices
this month only to be offset by some builders increasing discounts and promotional offers in response
to slowing demand. The annual pace eased to 4.7%yr. Dwelling repair costs were also a downside

surprise, falling 0.1%mth, their first decline since February 2025, although prices remain 3.4% higher
over the year. Some of this softness was offset by a firmer-than-expected increase in property rates
and charges, which rose 5.6%mth.
Recently, we have also focused on audio, visual and computing given the potential for spillovers from
strong global demand for semiconductors and other computing resources associated with the AI build
out. Prices for both equipment and services came in weaker than August, with equipment prices falling

1.1%mth, while services rose 0.3%mth. Despite this, annual price growth remains relatively high for
these categories, running at 4.6% and 4.9% respectively. Some of this pressure might also be showing

### Page 6

through in gaming, toys and hobbies, which is running at 5.2%mth/15.5%yr.
Overall, the breadth of inflation moderated for a second consecutive month. The share of the basket
running above 3%yr fell from 40% to 35%, meanwhile the share below 2%yr lifted from 27% to 32%.
Our replication of the San Francisco Fed’s inflation shock momentum served as a good guide to the
quarterly TM. With the full monthly reads in for the September quarter in, this measure does signal
some persistence/upside pressures for the quarterly TM going into the coming quarters. It is worth
cautioning that this measure will be impacted as the ABS refines its seasonal adjustment process for
the monthly indexes.

Compared to our expectations, the key surprises were:
Upside surprises
Downside surprises
Fruit & vegetables was the key upside surprise, 0.6%mth against our expectations for 3.7%mth fall.
Holiday travel & accommodation also surprised to the upside, following a run of downside surprises
since early this year. It rose 7.6%mth against our expectations for a 4.4%mth lift. 
Automotive fuel rose 9.4%mth against expectations of an 8.3%mth lift.

### Page 7

September Quarter CPI
Clothing & footwear was the key downside surprise with prices falling 2.7%mth against our
expectations for a 0.2%mth fall. The misses were broad-based: garments (–2.3%mth vs –2.0%mth);
footwear (–2.5%mth vs 1.8%mth); and accessories & clothing services (–4.1%mth vs 2.8%mth).
Household textiles declined 6.6%mth against our expectations for a 3.2%mth fall. Furniture &
furnishings rose by less-than-expected 0.4%mth vs 1.6%mth. Household appliances, utensils and

tools also came in softer, –3.2%mth vs –2.4%mth.
Dairy prices fell 1.2%mth against our expectations for a 0.1%mth lift.

### Page 8

The September quarter CPI printed spot on our expectation of 1.3%. The key factor driving this
increase was the slightly stronger surge in auto fuel (7.8%, +0.27ppt) and holiday travel &
accommodation (2.7%qtr, +0.16ppt) being offset by a slightly softer than expected increase in housing
(1.1%qtr, +0.24ppt) with rents and dwellings both increasing 1.0% in the quarter and utilities lifting
1.2%. Food was also slightly stronger than expected (0.7%qtr, +0.12ppt) and while not significant to the
overall result it is interesting to track the ongoing rise in motor vehicle prices (0.3%qtr) despite a very
competitive market with Chinese manufacturers rapidly gaining market share over legacy brands.

It is worth noting that food was stronger than we expected (0.7% vs 0.5%) with a robust 1.7% increase
in restaurant meals and a solid 1.2% increase in take away & fast foods outweighing the –0.4% decline
in fresh fruit & vegetables and meat & seafoods. Overall, market services ex volatile prices increased a
very solid 1.2%qtr but with the annual pace easing to 3.2%yr from 3.36%yr.
Outside of housing the other main downside surprise in the quarter was the –0.6% fall in clothing &
footwear (+0.3% expected) with prices falling across just about all categories of garments, footwear
and accessories.
September Quarter Trimmed Mean

### Page 9

Recall that the TM is calculated from seasonally adjust consumption expenditure classes so any
numbers quoted here may not be the same as the original estimates quoted from the CPI. The TM gain
of 1.0% in the quarter was slightly stronger than we had expected even though dwellings (which have a
weight of almost 11% in the TM) was a touch softer at 1.0% compared to the 1.1% we had pencilled in.
Rents (1.0%, 10% weight) also made a solid contribution as did restaurant meals (1.7%qr, 6% weight),

take away & fast food (1.2%, 4% weight), other financial service (1.0%, 6% weight). Domestic travel
was in the TM (1.0%, 4% weight) as was medical & hospital service (1.0%, weight 5%). The range of
the TM was from 0.0% (motor vehicles) to 1.7% (beer).
What does this mean for our December quarter forecast and into 2027?
We are working though the monthly results to see what changes they may imply for individual
component inflationary momentum. Our preliminary analysis suggests that the softer trend in dwellings

is not enough, on its own, to change our Q4 CPI forecast of 1.3%. It does, however, suggest that if all
else held equal (which is not the case as there is likely to be some offsetting stronger gains elsewhere)
the moderation in dwelling inflation presents a very modest downside risk our Q4 TM estimate of 0.9%.
We are still working the numbers and will publish an updated inflation profile as soon as we can. In
addition, the overall momentum in underlying inflationary pressures suggest the downside risk to the

TM from dwellings is more likely to appear in 2027 than in the last quarter of 2026.

## Document 474: USA_ Pushing Back Fed Rate Hike on Lower Core PCE Inflation; Q2 GDP Revised Up to +2.2%; Trade Deficit Widens More Than Expected; Lowering Q3 GDP Tracking to +3.3%

Source PDF: all report pdf/USA_ Pushing Back Fed Rate Hike on Lower Core PCE Inflation; Q2 GDP Revised Up to +2.2%; Trade Deficit Widens More Than Expected; Lowering Q3 GDP Tracking to +3.3%.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-09-30
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 2

Personal consumption (third estimate) +3.8% (qoq ar) for Q2 vs. GS +3.4%, median
forecast +3.4%, prior revised +0.7%
Personal income +0.2% for August vs. GS +0.5%, median forecast +0.5%, prior revised
+0.3%
Personal spending +0.9% for August vs. GS +0.8%, median forecast +0.9%, prior revised
+0.1%
Advance goods trade balance -$132.6bn for August vs. GS -$116.0bn, median forecast
-$115.3bn, prior revised -$118.9bn
ADP employment +90k for September vs. GS +75k, median forecast +75k, prior revised

+36k
MAIN POINTS:
1. The core PCE index increased +0.25% in August, somewhat below expectations, and
the year-over-year rate increased 3.01%. We estimate that the Dallas Fed’s trimmed
mean PCE measure increased 0.16% month over month and 2.20% year over year.
2. Year-over-year core PCE was revised downward 0.36pp in July to 2.98%, primarily
reflecting the methodological revisions to the calculation of the portfolio management
(-0.23pp), computer accessories and software (-0.11pp), and legal services (+0.01pp)

components of PCE. These downward revisions were larger than we expected, mostly
reflecting a larger revision to the portfolio management component than we expected.
3. Today’s inflation report, especially the downward revisions resulting from the
methodological changes, imply that core PCE inflation is likely to substantially
undershoot the median FOMC participant’s forecast of 3.4% for 2026 on a Q4/Q4 basis.
We now expect a 3.0% increase for core PCE in 2026 on a Q4/Q4 basis. Coupled with

comments from New York Fed President John Williams yesterday that “with the policy
action we took at our September meeting, there is no need for urgency, and we have
time to gather more information,” we now think that an October hike is unlikely.  We are
pushing back the second hike in our forecast to December, and we see a strong chance
that the FOMC will ultimately conclude that additional rate hikes are unnecessary.
4. Real GDP was revised up by 0.7pp to +2.2% in Q2 (quarter-over-quarter annualized),

largely reflecting upward revisions to consumption (+0.4pp to +3.8%) and investment
(+1.9pp to +4.6%) growth. The strength in consumer spending in Q2 likely continued to
reflect the boost to consumer cashflow from larger-than-usual tax refunds, but we
continue to expect consumer spending growth to slow somewhat in 2026Q4 as the fiscal
support fades and the headwind from higher energy prices remains. Stronger
investment growth reflected an upward revision to private fixed investment (+0.5pp to

+9.0%), driven by data center investment in the nonresidential structures category
(+1.9pp to +0.1%), and an upward revision to the contribution from inventories (+0.2pp
to -0.5pp).
5. Reflecting today’s annual revisions to the national accounts, real GDP was revised up
by 0.4pp to +2.5% in Q1 (quarter-over-quarter annualized), largely reflecting upward
revisions to the contributions from consumption and exports. Revisions for prior years
were modest: real GDP growth on a Q4/Q4 basis was unrevised for 2025 (+2.0%) and

2023 (+3.4%), and slightly revised down for 2024 (-0.2pp to +2.4%) and up for 2022
30 September 2026   2
Goldman Sachs

### Page 3

(+0.1pp to +1.3%). 
6. Personal income increased 0.2% in August, below expectations. Year-over-year 
disposable personal income growth reached 4.8% in August. Personal spending rose 
0.9% in August, in line with consensus expectations. The savings rate decreased 0.5pp to 
4.1% in August, after a large 1.6pp upward revision to the savings rate last month driven 
by an upward revision to the level of disposable personal income (+1.2pp contribution) 
and a downward revision to personal spending as a share of DPI (+0.4pp). 
7. According to the Advance Economic Indicators Report, the goods trade deficit 
widened by $13.7bn to $132.6bn (seasonally adjusted) in August, well above 
expectations. Goods exports increased by $3.7bn to $203.4bn, largely driven by an 
increase in industrial supplies exports. Goods imports increased by $17.4bn to 
$336.1bn, driven by a large increase in industrial supplies and capital goods imports. 
8. According to the ADP report, private sector employment rose by 90k in September 
(month-over-month, seasonally adjusted), slightly above consensus expectations. 
Employment in the services sector rose 59k, primarily reflecting a 55k increase in 
education and health services and a 22k increase in leisure and hospitality employment 
that were partly offset by a 16k decrease in financial activities employment. 
Employment in goods-producing industries increased by 31k, driven by a 17k increase in 
manufacturing and a 15k increase in construction employment. ADP’s new median 
annual base pay measure, which better tracks other measures of wage growth than its 
gross pay measure, increased 3.0% for job stayers, unchanged from the August pace, 
and 4.8% for job changers, 0.1pp above the August pace. We left our forecast for August 
nonfarm payroll growth unchanged at +80k (vs. +90k consensus) ahead of Friday’s 
release. 
9. The details of the personal income and spending report were firmer than our previous 
GDP tracking assumptions. While the trade-related details of the Advance Economic 
Indicators report were much weaker than our previous assumptions, suggesting notably 
stronger imports growth in Q3 than we previously assumed, the inventories details were 
significantly stronger. On net, we lowered our Q3 GDP tracking estimate by 0.1pp to 
+3.3% (quarter-over-quarter annualized).
